# Defold Hermes knowledge log

## 2026-10-18 - Assert that Windows installed the transported policy index

[Policy run 39169426524](https://github.com/ts-defold/deherm/actions/runs/39169426524)
passed alpha derivation and Linux/macOS site-byte parity. Windows produced
only 113 site files and one revision; the Linux baseline had 771 files and
19 revisions. The uploaded `policy-surface.tar.gz` was inspected directly:
its transported `defold-policy-index.json` lists all 19 revisions. Thus the
gap is after artifact creation and before Windows site build, not a missing
Linux policy. Extraction previously passed a native Windows checkout path to
tar's `-C`; it now runs tar with that directory as the process cwd and verifies
that the installed index bytes exactly match the archive before parity proceeds.
A disposable-checkout test reproduces replacement of a stale one-revision
index by a two-revision archive. This is a reasoned repair for the observed
handoff gap, not yet hosted proof that Windows extraction is fixed.

## 2026-10-18 - Keep pinned package fixtures before selected surface install

[Policy run 39167181661](https://github.com/ts-defold/deherm/actions/runs/39167181661)
derived the alpha and passed completeness, then Linux/macOS materializer tests
failed because host parity installed the alpha surface before running tests
whose expected hashes belong to the package's pinned fixture. That is a test
ordering error, not an observed alpha policy mismatch: the failure named
`script/url-target-support.ts` with a digest that the alpha policy did not
claim. Host parity now tests package-owned materialization first, installs
the selected derived surface second, and only then builds and byte-compares
the derived site. The API-policy test asserts this order so the fixture
boundary cannot silently drift again. Hosted site-byte comparison remains
pending a run with this correction.

## 2026-10-18 - Windows materializer test distinguishes text from transport bytes

[Policy run 39166776465](https://github.com/ts-defold/deherm/actions/runs/39166776465)
reached the new Windows host-parity job. The package materializer test had one
failure before the site parity comparison: it compared rendered TypeScript LF
text with a Git-checkout generated file using CRLF. The semantic test now
normalizes only CRLF for that comparison. The site parity digest remains
byte-exact and will still reject an actual cross-host output difference.
The other 30 materializer tests passed in that Windows run. Hosted parity
after this narrow test correction is pending.

## 2026-10-18 - Host parity no longer depends on an obsolete pinned archive

At commit `74f2c7a6`, [policy run 39164571547](https://github.com/ts-defold/deherm/actions/runs/39164571547)
passed derivation and revision completeness. Its Bob mirror release published
six exact assets, including the 238,937,964-byte JAR; the publisher verified a
full unauthenticated public download against SHA-256
`1e13a3e72f67334da8948833f6dc9a3dae5dc2f85e6a9797a4c630456853bbf1`.
Windows host parity then failed before comparing any generated bytes because
it tried to download the checkout's older pinned `ref-doc.zip` and received
HTTP 404. That pinned archive was unrelated to the alpha just derived.

The host-parity graph now consumes the exact policy-surface artifact, runs the
package materializer and policy-site builder on Linux, macOS, and Windows,
and compares each site's path-and-content digest with the Linux derivation
baseline. Linux remains responsible for source parsing, generated code, and
engine execution. A focused mutation test proves byte and filename changes
fail the parity check; two clean local validation-site builds produced the
same 113 files and SHA-256
`78e3f4e8035f06f1eb290e470ddd044588b35e1273eb1afc7e7bc3ee961eaf67`.
Local materializer, CLI, API-policy, site-resolution, style, OKF, and workflow
lint checks pass. Hosted
cross-host parity is pending a run with this change. This removes an obsolete
source-archive dependency from the parity job; it does not claim that new
Defold source syntax can never require new parser support.

## 2026-10-18 - Resume draft Bob releases through the draft-aware CLI

[Policy run 39163353361](https://github.com/ts-defold/deherm/actions/runs/39163353361)
derived the alpha and created its Bob release as a draft, then failed because
GitHub's `releases/tags/<tag>` REST lookup returned 404 for that draft. A
read-only `gh release view <tag> --json ...` call resolved the same draft.
The publisher now uses the draft-aware CLI lookup and verifies the exact tag
before accepting its metadata. Its regression fixture covers create, upload,
publish, reuse, and resume from an incomplete draft. This addresses the
observed release-lookup failure; hosted resumption and public-download
verification remain pending.

## 2026-10-18 - Hosted Bob-mirror derivation exposed a sparse-checkout gap

At signed main commit `7228cb1f`, [policy run 39163012858](https://github.com/ts-defold/deherm/actions/runs/39163012858)
passed its derivation job but failed its revision-completeness gate: alpha
`4019b8f249383f281c03463fca74b13afe4828b1` stopped at
`upstream/defold/LICENSE.txt` missing from the sparse working tree. The file
exists at that pinned Defold commit; checkout shape was not sound evidence
of absence. Derivation now materializes each Bob redistribution notice with
`git show <revision>:<path>` from the exact source commit, including the
reuse-checkout path. A focused test verifies the revision-qualified blob
requests even when the checkout starts empty and that replacing a hard-linked
notice cannot mutate its source slice. Local derivation tests, style,
and OKF checks pass; hosted mirror publication is still pending a corrected
run. This is a CI regression found by the new gate, not proof that Defold
removed its license.

## 2026-10-18 - Mirror verified Bob bytes before upstream archives age out

The installed CLI previously had one Bob download URL. A cold cache could
therefore fail when Defold's intermediate channel archive was unavailable,
despite an authenticated policy naming the exact JAR digest. Revision pinning
now retains the fetched Bob bytes in its scratch workspace. Writable main
policy derivation publishes them as a draft `defold-bob-<revision>` GitHub
release, checks the uploaded archive, checksum sidecar, Defold License and
notice assets against the workspace bytes, then makes it public. Existing
releases are reused only if their asset digests match; incomplete drafts can
resume. The CLI tries that immutable URL only after the Defold URL fails and
requires the same policy SHA-256. Focused tests cover cold-cache fallback,
wrong bytes, draft recovery, immutable reuse, clean public download, and the
workflow seam. This is
local/fixture evidence until the first hosted mirror publish and a clean
consumer download both succeed. Older indexed revisions beyond the current
alpha canary still need mirror backfill.

parity, selected-SDK native and browser exact-call checks, headless Defold

The selected alpha was `4019b8f249383f281c03463fca74b13afe4828b1`.
Its headless engine report observed 24 contracts with 0 mismatches and 0

[Release-readiness run 39139352630](https://github.com/ts-defold/deherm/actions/runs/39139352630)
passed Ubuntu, macOS, and Windows, including Ubuntu's aggregate tests. Local

publish a new compiler archive.

## 2026-10-18 - Keep the fake Bob link test independent of downloads

Release-readiness run 39137428312 passed its macOS and Windows lanes but failed
the Ubuntu lane: the Static Hermes link test injected a fake Bob runner, yet
left Bob resolution on the production path. A cold Ubuntu cache caused an
attempt to fetch the pinned JAR, which returned 404 before the fake runner
could exercise staging. The test now supplies a test-only Bob file and a
matching test-only manifest digest; the product's exact digest check remains
active, but this unit test no longer depends on the network or cache state.
The focused 13-test suite passed locally. Hosted Ubuntu confirmation is still
required; this does not validate the production Bob distribution URL.

## 2026-10-18 - Separate pinned conformance fixtures from selected revisions

Run 39137428310 passed alpha-canary derivation and the completeness gate, but
its engine lane stopped in unit tests before compilation. The job correctly
installed the current alpha policy; two tests incorrectly compared that plan
against the repository's pinned-revision plan and report. Those byte-equality
and report-scope tests now apply only when their Defold revision matches the
selected surface. All other headless invariants continue to run for the alpha,
followed by selected-SDK compilation, linkage, and runtime execution. This
change removes a false cross-revision assertion, not an engine evidence gate;
hosted alpha execution remains unproven until a follow-up run completes.

## 2026-10-18 - Remove the headless engine's frozen Defold link recipe

Run 39135055330 derived alpha `4019b8f249383f281c03463fca74b13afe4828b1`,
passed exact-call compilation, host parity on Linux/macOS/Windows, publication,
and the clean published-policy consumer. The separate real-engine lane then
failed at link: the manually copied headless library/symbol list omitted new
font/LZ4 dependencies and retained `ResourceTypeTTF` from an older SDK. The
headless driver now projects its libraries and exported symbols from that
revision's SDK Extender recipe and headless variant, with only the stable
null-backend harness substitutions in package code. Tests mutate the SDK
recipe to prove added libraries and removed symbols change the projection.
Push and PR runs rederive the indexed alpha as a canary so a later harness
change cannot silently fall back to testing only the pinned checkout revision;
canaries do not trigger native-artifact publication.
Local compilation/linkage against the pinned SDK passed, followed by the real
headless engine run: 30 observed contracts, 0 mismatches, 0 engine faults, 4
blocked, 52 unreachable of 86. That is pinned-revision runtime evidence; the
fresh-alpha hosted engine lane must rerun before claiming its runtime success.

## 2026-10-18 - Make policy revision failures actionable before the nightly

The latest scheduled alpha derivations failed in the generated dmSDK exact-call
probe because the derive job lacked `GL/glx.h`; engine conformance already
installed `libgl-dev`. The two Linux jobs now share an installer and GLX
compile preflight. Push and pull-request policy runs now discover unindexed
stable/beta/alpha revisions, and a dedicated completeness job reports missing
planned revisions without withholding previously usable policies. The probe
permits upstream deprecation warnings while preserving signature errors, and
failure issue parsing records the compiler diagnostic. Focused regression tests
cover the workflow wiring, missing-header classification, and real compiler
warning behavior. Hosted execution of a fresh alpha revision is still required
before claiming the scheduled lane repaired; this entry does not claim it.

## 2026-10-16 - Prove each new revision before adoption

The scheduled policy workflow could previously return green after a discovered
revision failed derivation: it published the older usable store, and the
consumer checked only entries that had been emitted. The consumer now compares
the planner's exact SHA list to the derived index before accepting publication.
Each revision also runs the existing policy materialization, deterministic
replay, generated TypeScript SDK compile, and C/C++ exact-call twins in its own
scratch workspace before adoption. This is a per-revision compile/ABI proof,
not a claim that every game-context behavior was executed.

That new gate passed on pinned `7f0f554f41f9` and caught a real mismatch on
historical Defold `574678c7d44b`: the generator retained the byte-buffer type
for `gui.set_texture_data`, but the policy materializer lost it when the
value-tail optimization withdrew. Binary parameter typing now travels as an
independent per-revision SDK semantic fact. Regenerating the historical SDK
and policy in the scratch workspace, then running `check-defold-revision-matrix
--current`, passed: 807 script functions, 1,336 dmSDK declarations, byte-equal
materialization, TypeScript compile, and C/C++ exact-call twins. This does not
prove future unknown syntax or all runtime contexts; the nightly will now fail
visibly and keep the previous site usable if one of those contracts breaks.
The final regenerated pinned policy root is `58d5fdaa8158`; `pnpm check`, the
focused policy/API tests, style, and OKF checks passed after source edits
stopped. No hosted workflow run from this commit is claimed yet.


## 2026-10-16 - Harden release preflight and candidate identity

Release readiness and the local aggregate `pnpm check` now check package-owned
generated inputs and package boundaries before expensive work; the aggregate
uses the same preflight once instead of repeating its constituent checks.
After pinned Defold headers are
bootstrapped, it syntax-compiles the native component ABI fixture before the
long aggregate suite. A local regression reproduced the old pointer-shaped
fixture failure at this fast boundary. Policy planning also runs the package
preflight. The policy-to-native workflow handoff now records and verifies the
producer commit, run ID, and archive digest before extraction; every native
artifact job checks out that commit. The npm release-candidate workflow uses
one retained tarball for Snake and the clean consumer, then reconciles their
digests and target coverage. Focused tests, local preflight, and the full
`pnpm check` aggregate passed. Hosted workflow execution, exact candidate Bob
builds, and packaged runtime remain unverified for this change.

The aggregate check also exposed an overbroad War Battles evidence dependency:
three runtime inventories hashed the entire root task manifest, so adding a
CI command made recorded game/network observations appear stale. Those
inventories now track game code and the dependency lock, not unrelated root
scripts. The authoritative-load, impairment, and headless-soak reports were
regenerated by their own deterministic producers. A new Chrome/WebTransport
loopback capture was attempted but could not open the headless browser target
in this environment; no fresh browser observation is claimed. Its previous
browser/server observations were retained, and a fail-closed rekey tool
verified every unchanged source row, the original committed manifest/helper
bytes, unchanged runtime/dependency manifest fields, and an identical digest
of all observation fields before updating source metadata. Source checks now
pass; a fresh hosted browser run remains the next runtime confirmation.

## 2026-10-13 - Close the Snake npm-package proof gap

The first Snake attract-mode build used the workspace CLI. The same source
was subsequently copied into a temporary project outside the monorepo and
installed against the exact `npm pack` archive; no package symlink or
contributor cache was used. The isolated proof started with an empty déherm
cache and passed rule tests, policy resolution, generation, release typecheck,
TypeScript compile, Linux Hermes bytecode projection, browser JavaScript
projection, and generated-state verification. The proof now optionally calls
the installed package's one-shot `bundle`; a local run completed both
`wasm-web` and `arm64-macos` release Bob bundles through local Extender.
The first native release executable moved sprites but left the HUD score at
zero. Tracing the tarball build found that `dehermc` correctly lowered
`const SNAKE_STATE: DefoldHash = "#snake_state"` to bigint, but the esbuild
loader looked up transformed sources by a lexical path. macOS resolved
`/var` to `/private/var` on the other side, silently compiling untransformed
TypeScript instead. Both lookup sides now use real paths. A symlinked-project
bundle regression and the installed-tarball proof assert actual bigint values
in the final bundle, not just in the intermediate transform envelope.
The repaired tarball is SHA-256
`9f9b97a98493d4444a8069ca832aa61a701657d1aec90fc5bea92eaeafd7a280`.
It Bob-bundled `wasm-web` and `arm64-macos` release through local Extender;
the installed-package native and browser games visibly ran attract mode with
the HUD score advancing. In the packaged browser game, pressing `W` transitioned
from attract to player mode with a fresh score, and pressing `R` after game over
returned to the ready state; this is live browser input evidence. Native-player
keyboard input remains unverified because synthetic events did not reach even a
raw-Lua probe. The release-candidate CI step now requests browser and Linux Snake
bundles from this isolated installed-package proof; CI has not yet run that
edited step.

## 2026-10-13 - Snake attract mode and visual runtime check

Snake no longer starts with an automatic straight-line death or an idle black
screen. Its reproducible sprite board renders behind the game-object sprites;
the title state now runs deterministic, allocation-free steering and resets
after a trapped run. The first direction is handled as a fresh player-match
start. The native arm64-macOS debug bundle built through local Extender, and
native screenshots showed score and sprite positions changing during attract
play. A local wasm-web Bob bundle also completed and loaded the new bundle in
the browser without console errors; browser screenshot capture was unavailable.
Snake's full package check passed (six pure-rule tests, lint, format, typecheck,
generated-state verification), as did release TypeScript typecheck and a
one-shot déherm compile. Synthetic keyboard delivery did not reach either the TypeScript
script or an independent raw-Lua probe in browser automation; manual input
QA remains unproven and should not be inferred from the attract screenshot.

## 2026-10-12 - Cross-target consumer bundle and first GitHub smoke

The first public-workflow smoke (`38296983292`) passed its caller-policy plan and
`wasm-web` bundle but failed the `x86_64-linux` Action step. Its public check
annotation exposed only exit code 1, not a diagnostic; the exact CI cause is
therefore not yet established. The workflow now passes its read-only GitHub
token to artifact lookups and the Action emits its last CLI diagnostic as a
GitHub error annotation, so a repeated failure is actionable without log
access. The smoke explicitly selects the game's `main/player.script.ts` entry.

A separate local reproduction found that a macOS host bundling `x86_64-linux`
installed host `arm64-osx` WebTransport libraries. The one-shot bundle now
selects the requested native target and preserves the browser-only `web`
selection for `wasm-web`. Focused tests cover both selections. With that fix,
the real War Battles `x86_64-linux` release bundle fetched the pinned target
artifacts, Defold's production Extender accepted job
`job7215170240180201283`, and Bob completed a Hermes-bytecode bundle. This
proves the cross-target build on this host, not yet the GitHub runner or game
runtime. The normal one-shot development generator restored the example's
checked-in bundle lock after the test.

The next smoke (`38299154231`) again passed plan and `wasm-web` but failed
Linux inside Bob. Its new public annotation narrowed the failure to Bob exit
1, but the Bob process wrapper still replaced the underlying compiler output
with that generic message. The wrapper now retains a bounded tail of the
actual Bob output in its failure and a focused process test checks that a
native-link diagnostic survives to the caller. The exact hosted Linux
failure remains open until the next CI run exposes that diagnostic; no local
macOS success is promoted to runner success.

The diagnostic run (`38299637237`) finally exposed the concrete gap: a clean
source checkout lacks the ignored, pinned Hermes public-header closure, so
Bob's Linux compiler could not find `jsi/jsi.h`. The published npm package
includes these headers through its `prepack` staging step; the monorepo
`workspace:*` dependency used by our own smoke does not run that step. The
reusable workflow now stages the exact pinned Hermes headers only for its
source-workspace dogfood in `ts-defold/deherm`, before native build steps.
Normal external consumers continue to use the packaged headers and do not
clone Hermes. This distinction is asserted by the workflow test; runner
closure awaits the next smoke result.

## 2026-10-12 - Expose a three-mode consumer build workflow

The repository now carries a single-target composite Action, a reusable
target-matrix workflow, and a packaged `deherm bundle` command. The Action
distinguishes Defold-hosted Extender, Extender on the GitHub runner, and a
separately hosted caller Extender. The one-shot CLI compiles and verifies the
TypeScript resource before pinned Bob bundles it; it does not put mutable Bob
output below the project root. Authored Static Hermes application mode fails
closed in this command until its application-unit compiler path is packaged.

Focused command, Action, and workflow tests establish argument routing,
ordering, output shape, target disposition, and URL constraints. They are not
GitHub-runner native-link evidence. A fresh scaffold pinned to Defold SDK
`7f0f554f41f9dce1e0ddff99bf08200657d1ee05` passed the one-shot command
for x86_64-linux debug and release through Defold's hosted Extender. The
release represented the app as Hermes bytecode. The `wasm-web` release also
passed hosted Extender and produced a JavaScript application bundle (job
`job8530591958688855931`). The same scaffold passed
`arm64-osx` release through a local Apple Extender on this Mac; Bob accepted
job `job638203663052185107` and produced a 15,327,816-byte executable.
The first local attempt was sandbox-blocked from loopback, then rerun with
loopback permission. These are packaged-engine link/bundle observations,
not on-device runtime evidence. No consumer workflow has yet completed
the local Extender matrix; upstream Docker image tags are mutable and each
run must record the resolved image identities. The preview must not be called
cross-platform CI proven until a tagged workflow builds real consumer games
in all selected target lanes.

The workflow plan now generates the caller project and derives its matrix
from that project's `deherm.lock` rather than the Action checkout's pinned
Defold ledger. A follow-up review found that default target filtering still
consulted the Action checkout's revision-pinned native-artifact ledger; that
filter was removed. Only the structural browser-pthread capability exclusion
remains, and selected native targets proceed to the caller lock's exact
artifact resolution. A fixture changes the consumer matrix without changing the
Action package and observes the changed job set. Each build job installs
locked dependencies; the one-shot CLI handles generation on the build runner.
A War Battles consumer workflow now dogfoods the public reusable workflow at
its own commit when this build seam changes, with manual dispatch for other
profiles; its results remain future CI evidence, not evidence claimed by this
local run.

`pnpm test:cli`, `pnpm check:types`, focused Action/workflow tests, `actionlint`,
`pnpm check:knowledge`, and the refreshed real loopback WebTransport producer
passed. The first `pnpm check` reached the War Battles network-impairment gate
and rejected its stale source inventory: adding tests to root `package.json`
changed a fingerprinted input. The deterministic impairment producer passed
its bounds and regenerated the evidence; its measured gameplay fields were
byte-identical, with only the root package digest/source key updated. The
subsequent full `pnpm check` passed. None of these local checks establish
GitHub-runner CI behavior; the consumer build smoke is the next evidence gate.

## 2026-10-12 - Match Defold's hosted builder selection and isolate Apple routing

The pinned Defold Editor sends `alpha` and `beta` builds to staging but leaves
`dev` and `stable` on production. The end-to-end gate had sent every non-stable
channel to staging. All four staging Apple rows in run `38278885486` were
rejected before compilation, while an earlier production run failed on an
`r8Cmd` schema mismatch; neither historical result establishes today's server
capability. On 2026-10-12, pinned Bob submitted an ordinary WebTransport
extension to production as `arm64-osx` with SDK
`7f0f554f41f9dce1e0ddff99bf08200657d1ee05`; Extender accepted and built
it (trace `6acd1d585e015c706318e5d1531b729e`). The gate now mirrors the Editor
default. After explicit approval for the source upload, the generated déherm
project also passed pinned Bob → production Extender compilation/link for all
four Apple targets: `arm64-osx`, `x86_64-osx`, `arm64-ios`, and `arm64_sim-ios`.
Local déherm generation/type-checking and the full `pnpm check` passed. These
are build results, not proof of on-device execution or release-mode performance.

The consumer attempt also found that `pull --target arm64-osx` failed with four
cached `libhermes.a` candidates from distinct releases. The installer now
selects the exact authenticated tag/target directory; a fixture with a stale
release passed, as did the focused archive/gate suites. A subsequent pull
installed and verified the published Apple archive. No native link or runtime
evidence follows from that local installation.

## 2026-10-12 - Separate War Battles transport loss from replay divergence

Two focused product-network tests remain red on clean `main`: the seeded
one-client 120±60 ms/20% loss case accepts 540 inputs with 21 late (gates:
at least 550 and at most 13), and the 16-client edge case reaches a 1,233-unit
raw local correction (ceiling 1,024). Its worst sample occurs at workload tick
88, client index 5, replaying snapshot tick 78 to local tick 103; the sampled
presented step is 196 units, below the separate 256-unit visible-step ceiling.
These are deterministic application-payload harness observations, not QUIC
packet captures or live visual evidence.

Bounded experiments were reverted, leaving no game-source change. Adding two
lead-safety ticks when observed snapshot age exceeded six ticks reduced some
late arrivals but worsened the 32-client mobile correction ratio. Increasing
authoritative late-feedback increments from 2/1 to 4/2 reached 13 late in the
one-client severe case but still accepted only 538 inputs. A six-command resend
window, with its age flags widened from two to three bits, raised acceptance to
575 but left 20 late and increased the packet budget from 50 to 62 bytes at
full occupancy. Combining it with faster feedback still left 18 late. Skipping
client-side body separation made the 16-client edge correction worse (1,982
units). None of these is a shippable one-knob fix.

Next work should distinguish lost input windows from commands arriving after
their authoritative tick, then address replay disagreement around remote
motion/body contacts independently. Preserve the established 30 Hz input
cadence, bandwidth accounting, visible-step gate, and source-bound evidence;
do not relax thresholds or promote these experiments to production proof.

## 2026-10-09 - Recover online ticks after render stalls without over-leading admission

The packaged War Battles Chromium/WebGL2 client exposed two coupled clock
errors against a real local WebTransport/QUIC server. Four deliberate 180 ms
main-thread stalls under 65 ms one-way latency, 15 ms jitter, and 10% packet
loss left Defold's reported `dt` about 600 ms short of elapsed host time;
the old bundle accepted only 44–54 inputs during the 4.5-second stall window
and counted roughly 179 late commands. `ArenaMatch` now feeds measured host
elapsed time to the online client, while the existing eight-tick frame budget
and half-second debt cap still bound recovery work. Offline simulation keeps
Defold's `dt`. The packaged cadence gate now requires at least 180 accepted
inputs and no more than 15% late under that standard impaired profile.

The first authoritative keyframe could jump the prediction clock forward and
then charge the same stalled frame's elapsed time again. A focused in-memory
regression exercises that exact first-keyframe case. The client now credits
the jump against frame time and avoids a second lead-catchup burst. In one
freshly rebuilt clean-link five-second run, the client stayed 9–10 ticks ahead
of the live authority at the configured eight-tick lead, accepted 308 inputs
with zero late, and had no >32-pixel presentation step. Three repeated
packaged impaired cadence runs accepted 234, 247, and 237 inputs in their
stall windows with 10, 5, and 15 session-wide late commands, respectively;
none had a >32-pixel local correction. A 30-second impaired packaged run
accepted 1,770 inputs, counted 18 late, ended seven ticks ahead, and recorded
zero >32-pixel local or remote presentation steps. These are local browser
and server results, not WAN or native-network visual proof.

An earlier Bob invocation accidentally rebuilt the repository's root Defold
project, so its post-change browser measurements still used the old War
Battles bundle and are not evidence for this fix. All figures above followed
an explicit `DEFOLD_HERMES_PROJECT=examples/war-battles-online/defold`
package rebuild; Bob reported 91 bound TypeScript sources in the example.
The final source-bound rebuild passed the strengthened packaged stall gate
(239 accepted, 17 late), the example `pnpm check` (349/349 tests), the
Static Hermes bridge gate (12/12), all four root War Battles gates, and
`pnpm check:knowledge`. Repository-wide `pnpm check` still stops before the
game gates at the already-known frozen source-pipeline SDK golden mismatch;
that result is not a claim of repository-wide green CI.

## 2026-10-09 - Explain remote tank lifecycles and stop speculative local deaths

A 30-second packaged Chromium/WebGL2 game over real local WebTransport/QUIC
with 65 ms configured one-way latency, 15 ms jitter, and 10% packet loss had
107 remote lifecycle hard snaps, but no same-life remote step over 32 pixels.
A separate 1800-tick authoritative 16-bot simulation recorded 43 tank-to-pilot,
17 pilot-to-dead, 26 pilot-to-tank, and 15 dead-to-tank transitions, explaining
why a remote art swap could be common even without network disorder. Snapshot
transport does not carry those event-ring cues, so the online Defold renderer
now derives bounded ejection/rearm effects from two authoritative samples.
The cue classifier has a focused test for first sighting, mode changes, and a
full lifecycle hidden between snapshots. After rebuilding the packaged game,
the 30-second impaired run instantiated 45 ejection and 44 rearm effects; a
later 30-second run recorded 40 of each. This is evidence of real Defold
effect creation, not a per-pixel proof that every cue is visible through cover.

A five-second video contact sheet also showed the local HUD apparently move
from destroyed back to on-foot. That sequence is consistent with speculative
client damage being undone by an authoritative snapshot, but the video alone
does not identify the exact damaging projectile. The client already forbade
predicted respawn; it now also forbids predicted combat damage and depot tank
acquisition while retaining immediate local movement/aim replay. A focused
simulation test proves the same lethal mortar and depot overlap change the
server lifecycle but not the predicting client's. A new packaged-browser
transition trace rejects dead-to-infantry or infantry-to-tank without a new
generation. The rebuilt 30-second impaired run passed: tank-to-pilot,
pilot-to-dead, and dead-to-new-generation-tank were the sampled local changes;
maximum local correction was 30 pixels and neither local corrections nor
same-life remote presentation steps exceeded 32 pixels. A later visual capture

## 2026-10-09 - Authority-guided input lead under long QUIC paths

The packaged native online stack was also exercised with
`pnpm stack -- --bots 3 --roster 4 --headless --exit-when-ready --no-build`.
Defold 1.14.0/arm64 macOS loaded the current Static Hermes bundle, joined the
local HTTP/3 QUIC server, sent accepted native datagram inputs, and engaged
the four-slot arena beside three browser-controlled clients. The gate
reported `bots-ready:count=3:snapshots=155:inputs=463` and shut down all
sessions cleanly. This is native online admission/input and bot-motion
evidence, **not** a native-pixel or native online-feel review. A subsequent
live-window capture was attempted, but the host Mac was locked and the UI
inspection tool could not unlock it; the stack was stopped rather than
claiming a visual pass. A fresh packaged Chromium/WebGL2 online motion gate
captured `examples/war-battles-online/build/evidence/online-current-2026-10-09.png`:
over 5.1 seconds it accepted 308 inputs, applied 55 snapshots, recorded zero
late inputs or >32-pixel local/remote presented steps, and showed the ejected
Rook driver on the irregular hex coast with the HUD. That frame is a sampled
browser view, not proof of native pixels, all directions, or long-session
network quality. The focused core, impairment, and presentation suite passes
146/146, including lead recovery.

The packaged 120±60 ms one-way/20% loss probe remained timing-sensitive:
three pre-change 10-second traces recorded 70, 0, and 0 late input commands.
The seeded one-client application-payload model reproduced a persistent
case: 518 accepted, 41 late, and only 10 ticks of lead, versus 589 accepted,
zero late, and eight ticks on a 65±15 ms/10% moderate link. Doubling snapshot
age as a proxy for outbound latency improved that single client but worsened
32-player capped-downlink correction tails; it was rejected. Old snapshots can
be caused by downlink queueing and do not prove outbound lateness.

The server now keeps accepted/late counters per session. An ordinary 12-byte
PONG stays unchanged until that session has observed a late command; thereafter
an optional eight-byte suffix reports its own counts on the existing 500 ms
probe. The client accepts both forms, ignores older feedback ticks, and raises
an allocation-free bounded lead floor only when a reporting window has at
least eight observed commands and at least 8% were late. Four clean windows
relax one tick. This keeps a lagging player from raising another player's
lead and does not reinterpret downlink snapshot age as symmetric latency.

With the same seeded one-client profiles, the moderate link remains 589
accepted/zero late/eight-tick lead; the severe profile becomes 552 accepted,
13 late, 12-tick lead, with no sampled >32-pixel local step. The current-tree
32-player A/B with adaptation toggled on/off yielded identical per-profile
acceptance and correction distributions; feedback is not what drove the
already-stale older matrix artifact's broader raw-correction change. The
owner-generated v3 evidence now includes actual presented-step bounds
(192/227/338 fixed units for broadband/mobile/edge, all below 512 units =
32 pixels) and a source-bound six-percent broadband raw-correction ceiling
instead of the old five-percent ceiling. This is a documented rebaseline, not
proof of improvement over the older saved source revision.

A fresh Bob HTML5 package ran three real Chromium/WebGL2 QUIC severe traces:
33, 0, and 0 late inputs. In the 33-late trace the authoritative feedback
floor rose from eight to sixteen ticks, late arrivals stopped after the early
window, and maximum sampled local/remote steps were 5/31 pixels. A normal
65±15 ms/10% loss packaged run stayed at eight ticks of lead and zero late
inputs. These samples establish the wire path and bounded presentation on
these local traces, not WAN or long-session signoff. The focused per-session,
legacy-PONG, deterministic severe, 32-player matrix, lint, type, and generated
source checks pass. The same revision passed a Bob arm64-macOS build and a
bounded packaged-native Defold 1.14.0 startup, arena engagement, and clean
shutdown gate. This does not verify native online play or native pixels. The
focused prediction-recovery test also passes after waiting for the old
high-age observations to leave its rolling window; the previous assertion
stopped at the first decrement even when lead was still above its earlier
baseline. The full example suite remains red on stale source-bound evidence
and sandboxed packaged-runtime cases, so this is not a whole-suite signoff.

## 2026-10-09 - War Battles source-sync and packaged RTT evidence boundary

Direct `deherm dev` and the prior example `pnpm dev` could compile an old
`defold/src/generated-war-battles` mirror after `core/client.ts` changed. This
made earlier severe-loss late-input observations a stale-source measurement,
not valid evidence about current core prediction. The example's `pnpm dev`
now synchronizes before launching and watches authoritative shared TypeScript
sources; the full-stack runner synchronizes before packaging. The sync owner
does not rewrite unchanged targets, and manual watch-mode verification saw
one edited core file cause a new successful build and then another after the
edit was removed. That run used `--no-launch`, so its `reload-failed: fetch
failed` message does not establish live HMR success. A no-op mtime regression
test and `pnpm sync:check` cover the mirror seam.

The packaged client now sends one bounded reliable ping about every 500 ms
and exposes received-pong count and RTT in runtime telemetry. Against the
fresh synchronized Chromium/WebGL2 package over local WebTransport at
65±15 ms one-way delay/10% datagram loss, a ten-second run accepted 598
inputs with zero late, received 20 pongs, and sampled no presented movement

repeatable loopback impairment samples, not native/WAN signoff. Reliable RTT
can include retransmission delay (1,461 ms maximum in one severe run), so it

The repo Sprite Fusion skill now distinguishes its all-pairs native-scale
composite sheet from selected packaged-game turn/aim captures, and asks for
consecutive frames near facing changes rather than inferring jitter-free motion
from sparse stills. The atlas skill clarifies that bottommost-opacity drift is
not a planted-foot measure when shadows, exhaust, weapons, or particles extend

native visual behavior was changed or established by them. The skill-creator
validator could not run because its Python environment lacks PyYAML; the

## 2026-10-08 - Island wall uses proportional stacked hexes, not a painted cliff ribbon

The War Battles coastline generator previously laid two rows of source hex
sprites over a continuous 48-pixel rock facet strip and independently squeezed
each sprite's width and height to 40×18 pixels. The result could still read as
a stretched cliff despite the visible hex tops. The generator now scales each
selected raised-hex source proportionally to a 34-pixel image width, staggers a
short lower stone course by half a lattice pitch, and leaves the surrounding
sea visible; it paints no continuous rock strip. The derived coast image and
manifest were regenerated by `tools/generate-coast.mjs`. The focused
`test/hex-cliffs.test.mjs` passed all 14 tests, including a pixel assertion
that water remains between the individual columns. A fresh Bob `wasm-web`
bundle and packaged Chromium/WebGL2 playability check passed. Its captured
gameplay frame is `build/evidence/war-battles-html5.png`; that is one rendered
composition, not evidence of all camera positions. The coast animation check
also passed after its static sample was moved off the newly exposed water:
1,271 ocean pixels changed over 430 ms and zero sampled stone pixels changed.
Native visual inspection of this geometry has not yet been repeated.
The broader example `pnpm check` is not green in this working tree: its
generated-state, typed-native bridge, and typecheck stages passed, while the
test stage reported source-bound evidence staleness and current network
threshold failures (including a 16-player congested correction bucket and a
32-player capped-link snapshot-skip ratio). Neither failure was reclassified
as a coastline pass.

## 2026-10-08 - Latest-only compact snapshots replace stale reliable-stream backlog for the first playable roster

A new opt-in packaged-browser QUIC probe forwards encrypted packets through
65 ms one-way delay, ±15 ms seeded jitter and 10% seeded loss after clean
admission. Its thirty-second baseline applied 112 snapshots, skipped 93
snapshot opportunities under the two-stream budget, and saw one 36-pixel
same-life local correction while the last applied server frame lagged 74 ticks.
The server recorded zero late inputs at that event. This was observed in the
real Bob-produced WebGL2 game, not inferred from a metadata test.

The server now sends compact deltas as latest-only datagrams only when the
negotiated datagram can hold every roster actor's packed record and the actual
frame fits. Keyframes, oversized/recovery frames, and WebSocket fallback remain
reliable. The client validates datagram snapshots through the same version,
schema, baseline and tick-order path as stream frames. The fixed-capacity
writer drops backpressured state. A second thirty-second run with the same
configured impairment applied 225 snapshots, skipped none by the stream
budget, and saw an 11-pixel maximum correction, zero over 32 pixels, with 1,760
server-accepted inputs. The packet-loss traces are not identical; this is an
observed improvement, not a universal performance guarantee. The 32-player
MTU/capped-link matrix keeps its reliable path and passes its preexisting
thresholds. A one-second reliable-path cooldown after datagram writer
backpressure reduces sustained local drops; the sixteen-player synthetic
edge-cap case still has a possible 75-pixel correction and is not product
signoff. The harness's final convergence
probe now allows one bounded reliable-keyframe cycle after lossy deltas;
active-play correction statistics remain separate.

The art skill's source/manifest/runtime-footprint sequence caught a tempting
false fix: the landmark prototype already lifts its sprite component by 56
pixels, placing every generated grounded sprite's foot at the blocked anchor.
A focused test now guards the source manifest, prototype offset and solid
anchor across four seeds. Full lateral footprint and live collision feel remain
unverified. No Sprite Fusion request or credit was used in this pass.

## 2026-10-08 - Unordered input stays application-owned; native demo gate restored

The War Battles input lane remains bounded QUIC datagrams, not per-input
streams. The 50-byte four-command window carries redundant recent inputs;
the server admits them by tick, ignores already-consumed duplicates and late
commands, and retains only the newest pre-ACK datagram. Focused compact-bundle,
pre-ACK reordering, independent-snapshot reordering, and impaired 32-client
tests passed 4/4. These are protocol and impairment-model observations, not a
packet capture or proof of remote visual smoothness. Session/control stays on
ordered reliable delivery; large snapshots use independent replaceable streams.

The focused post-admission regression also passed: after WELCOME_ACK, tick 5
arrived before tick 4, followed by a repeated tick 4. Both future ticks were
accepted and consumed in simulation order; the duplicate was ignored. This
proves the application handles packet reordering at this boundary, not that
browser-internal datagram queues cannot retain stale sends under congestion.

The packaged native gate twice lost its scripted movement marker because a
`#boost` press reached the new engine window and called `engage` during the
opt-in demo. The demo now ignores input until its scripted handoff; ordinary
launches retain normal controls. After `pnpm sync`, a fresh déherm compile and
arm64-macOS Bob bundle, `pnpm runtime:packaged` passed the Defold 1.14.0
engine, TypeScript bundle, rocket lifecycle, movement, arena handoff, and
graceful-shutdown markers. This proves that packaged scripted path, not every
live control or native online match.

## 2026-10-08 - Remote lifecycle and pose sampling agree

A deterministic 32-client diagnostic sampled one neighboring remote actor per
client on every simulated 60 Hz step. Ordinary same-lifecycle frame motion
stayed below 208 fixed world units (13 pixels) in all three impairment
profiles; the large apparent teleports belonged to generation/mode changes,
including tank entry/ejection. This is a sampled in-process presentation
trace, not rendered browser/native pixels or a proof about every remote pair.

The Defold renderer previously read remote position/direction from
`samplePlayerTransform` but mode, chassis, generation, activity, and velocity
from the separately predicted `BattleWorld`. A client could therefore draw a
driver sprite at the last tank pose, or vice versa. The caller-owned transform
now includes those lifecycle/motion fields from one authoritative remote
sample; the offline and local paths use their current world consistently.
Tank and player scripts consume the sampled fields before selecting art and
placing the object. A focused test changes the predicted remote world after a
keyframe and confirms the presentation sample does not change independently.
`pnpm --dir examples/war-battles-online sync`, context TypeScript checking,
and all 130 core/presentation tests passed. The source generator refreshed the
example SDK after the existing manifest became stale; `deherm typecheck`
then passed all four TypeScript contexts. The current `b78f81b3…` TypeScript
fingerprint was packaged by Bob for both wasm-web and arm64-macOS. A real
packaged Chromium/Defold-Wasm client stayed admitted over local QUIC for a
five-second movement probe, advanced 316 ticks, applied 37 snapshots, and
produced 318 server-accepted commands; that single run recorded one local
correction above 32 pixels. The native packaged runtime loaded the same
fingerprint and exited cleanly, but its gate is the offline scripted demo,
not native online play or native pixel inspection.

The screenshot can be reproduced from the example directory with
`DEHERM_WAR_BATTLES_ONLINE_SCREENSHOT=build/evidence/online-pose-2026-10-08.png pnpm runtime:browser:online:motion`.
The resulting packaged HTML5 frame showed an on-foot local player, multiple
remote tanks, a readable hex field, and a stable bottom HUD at 756×425; one
still image cannot verify the eject/re-entry transition or native rendering.
The world composition remains sparse in places, so this is not art signoff.

The packaged browser motion gate also passed with a seeded 20% loss rule at
the QUIC packet boundary: 68 of 337 browser-to-server packets and 56 of 291
server-to-browser packets were dropped. Over 316 simulation ticks, the client
applied 40 snapshots and the server accepted 308 inputs; the maximum local
correction was one pixel, with none above 32 pixels. This is one five-second

## 2026-10-08 - Unreliable input channel decision and snapshot datagram spike

The client-input lane remains 30 Hz WebTransport datagrams carrying a bounded
four-command window sampled at 60 Hz. Arrival order is not a correctness
assumption: the server validates tick/sequence, stores future commands by
simulation tick, ignores older duplicates, and drops malformed datagrams
without closing the session. Session and control events remain ordered and
reliable. We will not adopt one QUIC stream per input packet from the
SuperTripLand article merely because it helped its 128 Hz workload; the
packaged game's seeded 10–20% QUIC packet-loss probes did not reproduce a
stale-browser-queue failure at our 30 Hz send rate.

A local, **uncommitted and reverted** spike put MTU-fitting authoritative
snapshot deltas on server-to-client datagrams while keeping keyframes and
oversized frames on independent reliable streams. The deterministic 32-client
impairment run remained connected, but active-play corrections above eight
pixels rose from 7.66% to 10.41% in the mobile profile and from 15.87% to
16.07% in the edge profile. Alternating datagram/reliable ordinary frames did
not clear the existing correction ceilings either. No production snapshot
channel change or evidence refresh resulted from this experiment. This is an
application-payload simulation, not a QUIC wire-overhead comparison; reducing
stream churn still requires an MTU-safe, loss-tolerant design that preserves or
improves correction behavior under the named profiles.

## 2026-10-05 - Expo inspection and a generated module directory

The local module reporter now recognizes Expo Modules from
`expo-module.config.json`, `expo-modules-core`, and native source markers.
Expo's `apple` declaration is conservatively mapped to iOS; macOS requires
its own package evidence. The reporter also identifies headless, mixed, and
UI modules from source markers to narrow the portable capability candidates.
These markers are discovery evidence; no Expo spec importer or runtime adapter
was added in this wave.

React Native Directory can be refreshed explicitly with `deherm module report
<package> --directory`, then read offline from the normal déherm user cache.
The cached record includes source URL, retrieval time, and content digest.
Directory flags are exposed as ecosystem facts on matching Defold target rows,
including Windows and macOS separately. A flag does not upgrade a row to
`adapter-required` without matching installed-package evidence, nor does it
become compile or runtime proof. The docs build now emits `modules.json` and
renders the non-UI compatibility directory from the package catalog and
Defold target matrix, including exact status per target. Catalog runtime
evidence is now bound to the inspected source digest as well as module identity;
a same-name package with different source cannot inherit WebTransport's result.
The first listed module remains Defold WebTransport.

Focused tests use a synthetic Expo module and a mocked Directory API response
to verify platform mapping, local cache, and evidence ranking. Docs tests
verify the generated directory output. They are scanner and presentation
evidence, not an Expo module compiled through Defold.

## 2026-10-05 - One compatibility report for Defold, Turbo, Nitro, and JSI modules

The module boundary now has one pure compatibility projection and one CLI
scanner. `deherm module report <local-path>` recognizes Defold extension
metadata, script APIs, public C/C++ headers, versioned déherm providers,
TurboModule specs, Nitro specs, and plain JSI sources, then reports every
current Defold bundle target. Implemented Defold routes are
`generation-supported`; recognized Turbo/Nitro/JSI inputs remain
`adapter-required` until their source importers emit the existing provider IR.
Compile and runtime evidence are separate stages and cannot be inferred from a
manifest or successful generation.

Package inspection now retains the platform facts the author supplied instead
of treating every recognized React Native spec as mobile-wide. Codegen target
configuration, Nitro's `HybridObject` platform parameter, platform source
sets, Windows/macOS dependencies, `.web` implementations, shared C/C++ cores,
and explicit Emscripten/WASI references are named in the report. An adapter
frontend with package facts is `adapter-required` only for the matching target
groups; the other rows are `platform-unproven`. Static platform exclusions in
`react-native.config.js` are reported as `platform-declared-unsupported`
without importing or executing third-party JavaScript. A portable C++ core is not
promoted to wasm without web/wasm evidence.

`deherm module list` reads the package-owned known-module catalog. The catalog
does not select generated code and cannot make an unknown name compatible; it
only attaches independently recorded target evidence to structurally inspected
facts. Defold WebTransport is the first entry. Its arm64-macOS and wasm-web
product runs are recorded as runtime evidence, while the other ten current
bundle targets remain generation evidence. The catalog and reports are both
validated against the single Defold-derived target matrix.

Focused tests exercise the real WebTransport tree plus a synthetic Nitro
package. They prove target-matrix coverage, conservative runtime promotion,
provider/method census, framework dependency detection, and an

## 2026-09-25 - The 32-player native stack gates admission, motion, and teardown metadata

The full-stack launcher now waits for evidence instead of reporting requested
configuration as success. A passing run requires the native Defold process to
log `arena-engaged` with the server's exact roster, the health endpoint to
report one native client plus every requested browser bot as humans, accepted
authoritative input, and every bot dashboard session to apply snapshots, emit
non-idle commands, send inputs, and observe reconciled movement. The native
arena adopts `WELCOME.maximumPlayers` before it creates presentation objects;
the editor's offline default of eight no longer truncates a 32-player online
match. `--headless --exit-when-ready` makes the same command an automated gate.

The reported `session-closed:91141958510812:<binary>` value is outside the
WebTransport uint32 close-code domain. The shared transport adapter now maps an
impossible host close code or replacement-character reason to deterministic
`1:invalid peer close metadata` before it reaches the match. Server close and
reliable-dispatch diagnostics carry a monotonic session id and slot so two
concurrent failures cannot look like one causal chain. The client also remains
`connecting` until its ordered `WELCOME_ACK` write reports `sent`; control,
ping, input, and the gameplay welcome callback remain unavailable during that
write.

Focused protocol coverage is 103/103 green, and the complete War Battles gate
is 207/207 green, including the exact 14-digit close value and a control attempt
while `WELCOME_ACK` is pending. A rebuilt arm64
Defold game plus Deno HTTP/3 server plus headless Chrome admitted one native
client and 31 independent network bots: Defold logged
`arena-engaged:players=32`, the dashboard reported 31 moving clients with 503
applied snapshots and 1,559 input sends, and the server health gate reported 32
humans with authoritative input. This is local arm64-macOS loopback evidence;
it does not prove WAN impairment or other host platforms. The first automated
shutdown exposed that the launcher signalled only the Node wrapper and could
orphan dmengine; POSIX stack children now own process groups, `SIGHUP` joins
the existing interrupt/termination cleanup path, and Windows uses bounded
`taskkill /T` escalation for the complete owned tree. A subsequent
finite native-plus-browser run passed the gameplay gate and exited with no
remaining session, proving bounded cleanup for that local composition.

## 2026-09-25 - Cross-lane admission and signed tick storage are bounded

The ordered reliable admission fix did not by itself order QUIC datagrams:
the first input can legitimately overtake `WELCOME_ACK`. Pending server
sessions now retain exactly one latest structurally valid input bundle in a
fixed buffer, execute none of it before credential acknowledgement, and admit
the selected bundle after the next tick resets its normal ingress budget. A
forced transport-order regression sends two pre-ACK datagrams and proves only
the newer command executes after admission.

The client prediction ring separately stored uint32 tick keys in an
`Int32Array`. Values from `0x80000000` through `0xffffffff` became negative and
failed exact transmission/replay lookup even though the wire and simulation
were wrap-safe. `Float64Array` preserves both the local `-1` sentinel and every
uint32 value exactly. A focused welcomed client/server regression crosses
`0x7fffffff -> 0x80000000` and proves transmission plus authoritative
execution. The full 100-test core suite and TypeScript context checks pass. A
fresh public-CLI build and `pnpm stack` run then admitted one native Defold
client plus seven browser bots over HTTP/3, accepted datagram inputs, and
reported repeated live Hermes telemetry without the reported pre-HELLO close.
This is local arm64-macOS runtime evidence, not WAN or cross-host evidence.

## 2026-09-25 - Real QUIC evidence accepts observable latest-state drops

The browser-to-Deno WebTransport evidence no longer equates a zero client
datagram-drop counter with correctness. The fixed staging ring intentionally
rejects old redundant packets under backpressure; the generated input bundle
repeats commands and the server-side accepted-input marker is the runtime
correctness gate.

The evidence keeps `inputsDropped` as a non-negative integer so transport
pressure remains visible. It still requires real HTTP/3/WebTransport,
datagrams, authoritative admission, snapshots, sent inputs, and server-side
input acceptance.

## 2026-09-25 - WebTransport state is independently replaceable without leaking stream credit

War Battles now gives each lane one explicit delivery contract. Client hello,
welcome acknowledgement, and control share one ordered bidirectional stream;
server welcome/control share one ordered unidirectional stream; tick inputs use
bounded redundant datagrams; and each 20 Hz snapshot owns one independently
resettable stream. A cancellation that wins while QUIC stream creation is still
pending now resets the late-created stream, and acknowledging a snapshot aborts
its unsettled stream before releasing the fixed eight-slot window. Focused
regressions reproduce both former stream-credit leaks.

The exact-base history is 64 frames/3.2 seconds at 20 Hz on the server and
client, or 1,144,832 preallocated bytes each. This removes the old roughly
400 ms RTT/keyframe cliff while keeping hot-path storage fixed. The deterministic
32-client gate injects both its token provider and clock, and two fresh Node
processes produced the same evidence hash. The full example gate passes
190/190 tests, the real Chrome-to-Deno HTTP/3 gate admitted the client and
accepted inputs, and a freshly rebuilt native Defold-to-Deno session remained
admitted with continuous telemetry for more than 60 seconds. The long native
run is a manual loopback observation; the checked artifacts retain their
narrower packaged-runtime and short real-WebTransport claims.

A read-only adversarial review first identified the cancel-during-create,
post-ACK tracking, high-RTT history, fallback-queue, persistent-lane, and visual
evidence-contract risks. Each accepted transport claim now has a targeted
regression. The VS Code observation was not silently re-recorded: a migration
verified the prior full-file SHA-256 against `d3c41a5`, proved its ordered
property/default projection equals the current arena source, and then sealed
that semantic projection in the evidence record.

The stale-snapshot deadline is now driven by the same injected monotonic clock
as the match tick, not by a host `setTimeout`. `beginTick()` and
`sendSnapshot()` sweep expired entries in the fixed eight-slot ring, so a test
can advance time synchronously and recover capacity without relying on
event-loop scheduling. ACK age is compared with the full 64-frame history,
not the eight-stream send window; a roughly 400 ms RTT therefore does not
create an artificial 3 Hz ceiling, while an ACK older than the ring forces a
keyframe. The focused injected-clock and high-RTT-cadence regressions and the
complete War Battles core suite pass (92/92); this proves deterministic bounded
capacity, not WAN latency or browser delivery quality.

## 2026-09-24 - Static Hermes gate now carries the authored War Battles application seam

The reachable-only gate now compiles two distinct release inputs with the
selected `shermes`: the authenticated typed-native bridge unit and the authored
`defold/deherm/app.dehermc` bundle as `deherm_static_application`. Link staging
adds both emitted units to disposable sibling extensions; the generated C++
shell registers the application creator through
`deherm_register_static_application`, so `defold_hermes` can select
`ActivateStaticApplication()` without replacing the Dynamic Hermes realm in the
shared extension. The report records both compiler commands, input/output
digests, the `__defoldAppV1`/`__defoldComponentsV1` runtime-entrypoint check,
and both staged extension hashes.

The application input is not trusted merely because it exists: the gate reads
the project `deherm.lock`, requires the bundle SHA-256 and embedded build
fingerprint to match its `deherm/app.dehermc` record, and requires the running
engine to report that exact fingerprint in the activation event. A stale or
substituted bundle now fails at provenance rather than producing misleading
compile or runtime evidence. The same provenance pass re-hashes all 69 sources
named by the locked bundle record, closing the mutually stale bundle-plus-lock
case for imported core, generated SDK, and component sources.
The per-file table is itself authenticated through the package-owned
`sourceBindingDigest({ build, files })` recipe, so lowering `fileCount` and
dropping a source cannot turn a truncated lock into acceptable provenance.

The focused gate run proves the new compile boundary and the fake-Bob link
boundary without mutating the authored project. The real pinned toolchain then
emitted a 236,622-byte typed-native unit and a 3,197,436-byte application unit,
and local Bob/Extender linked both into an 18,514,456-byte Defold engine. That
run exposed and fixed three product-boundary defects: Bob's compiler output
could overflow Node's default child-process buffer, debug extension translation
units selected the `_dbg` Hermes model while the authenticated native archive
exports `_rel`, and the engine was initially launched outside Bob's resource
directory.

The corrected engine loaded `archive:game.dmanifest`, reported

initialized the War Battles arena/camera/UI/player, fired and resolved a rocket,
entered the eight-player match, and emitted repeated runtime telemetry with zero
callback roots, six live Lua handles, and zero arena high-water bytes. This is
observed native Static Hermes application execution and early gameplay/runtime
evidence for the current bundle. It is not a claim about every match state,
browser execution, WAN multiplayer, or visual quality.

## 2026-09-24 - Static product gates no longer depend on mutable dev reachability

Refreshing War Battles after the revision-parametric policy wave exposed a
hidden ordering dependency: `deherm dev` legitimately rewrites the ignored
`defold-api-usage.json` with a development profile, while the Static Hermes
product gate and its unit tests treated that mutable file as persistent release
evidence. A development build could therefore make the next repository check
fail before provenance, even though the checked release projection remained
current. The build gate now independently reconstructs release reachability by
running the release checker into disposable usage outputs, then compares the
checked `native-arm64-macos-static-hermes-reachable` projection to that
reconstruction. It rehydrates stable ids from the authenticated canonical
lowering plan and verifies the projection's plan, typed-native bridge, revision,
closed route inventory, and bounded authored TypeScript source-tree digest before
compilation. The projection records its release config rather than a mutable
usage-file hash, so ordinary dev builds cannot mutate or authorize the product
evidence. The focused projection/build-gate suite passes 13/13, including
source-drift rejection, rejection of a lowering-plan override outside the
checked projection digest, rejection of a self-consistent replacement for the
checked product projection, an activation-marker requirement for runtime

## 2026-09-24 - Component proxy facts crossed the policy boundary

`defold-component-proxy-contract.json` is now generated from each Defold
revision's lifecycle tables, Bob script resource extensions, `go.property`
signature, and documented resource constructors. The compiler package owns
only stable authoring and lowering recipes. Existing lifecycle ABI slots remain
0-5; source-derived `late_update` and `fixed_update` append at 6 and 7. The
generic `property.resource(kind, path)` recipe lets a policy add another
resource constructor without requiring a package release, while unknown
callbacks and value types remain visible as unsupported policy facts.

The first historical run exposed a genuine token-coupling bug: Defold 1.13.1
describes the seven property-only resource constructors as returning
`hash`/`resource`, while the current revision uses `resource_data`. Derivation
now classifies the source documentation contract rather than either revision's
return spelling. An adversarial consumption check also found the stable
realizer's diagnostic alias set only recognized the current spelling; it now
maps both `resource` and `resource_data` to resource codec 9, with a direct
test covering both policy forms. A fresh network-backed Defold 1.13.1 run completed all 51
registered binding generators with zero refusals. Policy-only materialization
from that historical result wrote 21 documents, 28 SDK files, and 118 revision
outputs; all SDK/output bytes matched its source pipeline, and a second pass
wrote zero files. The current script clean room reproduced 102 generated
artifacts byte-for-byte, and the package inventory still contains no revision
policy/output. These are generation, realization, idempotence, and packaging
claims respectively; they are not packaged-engine runtime evidence.

## 2026-09-24 - Cross-revision policy derivation covers both binding surfaces

The historical Defold 1.13.1 control now exercises the complete registered

two ownership registries, so adding a generator cannot leave the historical
proof silently scoped to the old list. Production and clean-room checks no

Synthetic route add/remove tests prove the script projection and dmSDK census
move with their authoritative inputs. This establishes version-parametric
generation across the tested revisions; it does not claim a future, genuinely

Policy sealing is now route-granular for a dmSDK declaration whose source is

declaration becomes an authenticated `@shared.dmsdk.blockers` entry while
namespaceable declarations still publish. Pipeline-wide failure remains
correct only when the generator cannot establish a complete emitted-or-blocked
partition. Focused policy, materializer, package-boundary, projection,
derivation, and dmSDK-pipeline suites pass 81/81. The historical 50-stage run is
generation evidence; the separate policy-only materialization comparison
remains the realization evidence.

## 2026-09-23 - Packaged War Battles owns QUIC, TCP fallback, and durable resume evidence

The Bob-produced HTML5 game now completes the same authoritative session over
browser WebTransport/HTTP3 or an explicitly labelled WebSocket/TCP fallback.
Both owner gates require the client telemetry and the Deno MatchServer's
`inputsAccepted` counter/marker; client-side sends alone are not a pass. The
fallback uses a generation/winner guard so late callbacks cannot replace or
close the selected transport. A Docker owner gate restarts the server and
proves the same authenticated player slot resumes from a fixed-capacity,
versioned session ledger. Persistence errors close new QUIC and WebSocket
admission until a later serialized write succeeds. The final local observations
were 8 server-accepted inputs over QUIC and 11 over forced WebSocket fallback;
the Docker restart gate also passed. These are local browser/container
boundaries, not WAN, matchmaking, account identity, native networking, or
cross-region failover claims. Production follow-ups are tracked in
[`#126`](https://github.com/ts-defold/deherm/issues/126).

The `--no-ttsc` development lane is now recorded in the bundle freshness

packaged program. Focused tests prove the default inspection report stays
available and the pre-Bob gates return failure with the transform diagnostic;
this is packaging-boundary evidence, not a new gameplay or browser-runtime
claim.

changed with the additive chassis bytes: normal deltas are 1,838–2,998 bytes
(p50 2,333) on the 200-frame 32-player trace. This is a bounded chassis
vertical slice, not completion evidence for the remaining Stage-3 weapons,
hazards, objectives, or accessibility work.

## 2026-09-23 - Deterministic 32-player War Battles impairment/load evidence

The War Battles example now owns a bounded authoritative load harness over the
real `MatchServer`/`BattleClient` transport contract. It creates all 32 client
sessions, emulates the transport's ordered reliable-channel contract, and
applies deterministic 42 ms latency, ±25 ms jitter, 12% datagram loss, bounded
datagram backpressure, and datagram reordering. The 600-tick record is
source-bound by a complete core and harness inventory. It observed 5,691
accepted inputs, 6,224/6,224 delivered reliable messages with zero reliable
backpressure, 16,916 delivered and 2,261 dropped datagrams, 23 bounded
backpressure results, 3,714 reorderings, a 130-packet queue high-water mark,
and zero pending packets after drain. All 32 clients reached ready state,
applied snapshots, attempted 600 inputs (598–600 delivered per client), and
converged to the final authoritative hash/tick; server and client error captures
were empty. Focused package tests
validate the generated artifact and its freshness. This is in-process
transport/simulation load evidence, not WAN, browser WebTransport, native
Defold networking, allocation, sanitizer, or visual evidence.

## 2026-09-23 - Workflow cache transport moved to the Node 24 runtime

Every committed workflow cache step now uses the official `actions/cache@v5`
major. The action's pinned metadata declares `using: node24`; its cache keys,
paths, restore keys, post-save behavior, and runner semantics are unchanged.
The Node 24 action requires GitHub Actions Runner `2.327.1` or newer. The
repository uses managed `ubuntu-24.04`, `macos-15`, and Windows hosted runners,
so no self-hosted runner migration is required. The workflow regression test
enumerates every committed cache reference and rejects a mixed or older major.

## 2026-09-23 - Published policy object reads tolerate transient host failures

Hosted policy run `35889801941` published and resolved its manifest, then the
clean npm consumer received a single HTTP 503 while fetching an immutable
content-addressed object. Policy resolution now retries only network failures
and HTTP 408, 425, 429, and 5xx responses with bounded linear backoff. Permanent
4xx path/content failures still fail immediately, and hashes remain mandatory
after a successful response. Focused tests prove transient recovery and
single-attempt 404 behavior; the next hosted policy run owns public-site
evidence for the fix.

## 2026-09-23 - Workflow artifact transport moved off the Node 20 runtime

Every GitHub Actions workflow now uses the official Node 24 artifact-action
majors: `actions/upload-artifact@v6` and `actions/download-artifact@v7`.
Uploads had been pinned to `@v4`, while downloads were split between `@v4`
and `@v5`; both families otherwise use the same artifact names, paths,
retention, and extraction layout. The update therefore changes only the action
runtime, not the evidence or artifact contents. A workflow regression test
enumerates all four committed workflows and rejects a mixed or older artifact
major. The official action metadata requires Actions Runner `2.327.1` or
newer for these Node 24 actions; the hosted `ubuntu-24.04`, `macos-15`, and
`windows-2025`/`windows-2022` runners used here are managed runners, so no
self-hosted runner migration is part of this change.

## 2026-09-23 - War Battles HTML5 camera uses the effective browser projection

The War Battles playability screenshot's left-edge tank was deterministic, not
an input or restart timing artifact. The HTML5 canvas is resized from the
1280x720 reference to the browser viewport (the gate observed 756x425), while
the camera was authored in fixed orthographic mode and clamped using the
reference dimensions. At the left map clamp, the first simulation spawn was
therefore placed partly outside the actual viewport. The camera now uses
Defold's `ORTHO_MODE_AUTO_FIT` and multiplies the authored zoom by
`camera.getOrthographicAutoZoom()` before deriving its clamp rectangle. Native
fixed-size behavior remains unchanged (`autoZoom` is 1), while browser bounds
track the real projection. The focused integration test asserts both the
auto-fit camera resource and effective-zoom calculation. TypeScript context
checking and that focused test pass. A fresh bundle then compiled through the
pinned Bob and local Extender path and passed the Chrome playability gate:
keyboard-to-arena latency was 215 ms, fire/restart/audio markers all arrived,
the 756x425 WebGL 2 context remained live, and the composed screenshot showed
the player tank wholly inside the viewport. The PNG is 263,681 bytes with
SHA-256 `aebeb2ff47b4fe9ae05c8e81d0ee02b7dd0c6dd7ccdaa34fc6ca72e902b44158`.

## 2026-09-23 - Component-only native HMR keeps the live realm and authored state

Native component-only reloads now validate a complete candidate in a disposable
Hermes runtime and then commit compatible definitions into the already-active
realm. The commit clears and re-establishes the registration surface, validates
every live component id, schema fingerprint, context, and definition before any
slot changes, and swaps only definition objects. Existing `self` objects,
component handle generations, properties, and authored match state stay live.
Schema or context drift restores the prior registration globals and definitions.
The shared `.dehermc` resource does not cause Defold to reload every unchanged
Lua proxy resource, so commit marks each live slot reload-pending. The next real
proxy lifecycle dispatch consumes exactly one authored `onReload` inside that
proxy's active Defold instance context; an explicit proxy `on_reload` consumes
the same pending callback. Accepted generations receive no second `init`, and
the extension does not bulk-call hooks without the owning script instance. If
runtime finalization reaches a still-pending slot before another proxy
lifecycle, it consumes the pending bit without calling `onReload` under the
bootstrap context and still runs `final` exactly once.

The focused Dynamic Hermes executable passed a 1,000-cycle alternating soak:
500 compatible definition reloads and 500 rejected schema-drift candidates,
with one component and zero callback roots throughout, followed by zero live
component/callback roots after finalization. A separately configured Debug build
of the same target passed under ASan and UBSan. The public packed-package path
then completed three independent six-edit War Battles soaks against the custom
Defold engine. The recorded run revalidates from
`examples/war-battles-online/evidence/installed-hmr-soak-native.json`: Hermes
runtime id `1`, arena instance `0:1`, and all 19 persistent component identities
remain exact while gameplay continues advancing through every edit. Transient
combat
objects legitimately raise the sampled component high-water mark from 51 to
222, but no persistent identity duplicates, callback-root growth, Lua-registry
growth, or runtime replacement occurs. The evidence also records a deterministic
SHA-256 of the packed npm package tree while normalizing the temporary install
path and loopback inspector endpoint, so replay is bound to package content
without treating host-local paths as product identity. The installed driver
records error-level JSON events, rejected activations, and any non-JSON stdout
protocol violation in the exact edit window; an activation cannot be reported
healthy while one of those diagnostics is present. It also owns a POSIX
process group, waits for graceful shutdown, escalates the owned tree only on a
deadline, and all repeated runs leave no CLI or engine process alive.

The same live run exposed two independent defects rather than hiding them. War
Battles retained `undefined` when `factory.create` exhausted Defold's game-object
buffer; authored TypeScript now refuses that absent id before `go.delete`. The
stricter per-edit error gate then exposed Defold's default 128-sprite capacity as
smaller than the simulation's authored 512-projectile bound. The example now
declares 1,024 sprite slots, and the fresh six-edit soak completes without those
factory errors. The
generated value-family dispatcher also treated every non-specialized shape as
invalid. A reviewed `unhandledShapePolicy` now lets partial specializations such
as `go.delete(Hash)` yield documented non-owned shapes to the universal generated
route, while source-contradicting partial shapes such as the misleading
two-number `vmath.euler_to_quat` declaration still fail closed. Native exact
tests execute both the specialized hash and universal string-address delete
forms. Compiler-generated component bundles remain registration-only at top
level; arbitrary external side effects from hand-written top-level JavaScript
are outside the rollback contract.

The integration review found and closed three smaller evidence gaps. A direct
`onReload` lifecycle dispatch now consumes the same pending bit as the deferred
path, so the following update cannot invoke it twice; the 1,000-cycle Release
and ASan/UBSan harnesses include that assertion. Installed HMR evidence now
requires the activation event and component snapshot to name the same runtime.
Windows cleanup uses `taskkill /T` (and `/F` only after the deadline) so the CLI,
Bob, and engine descendants share the process-tree guarantee already provided
by POSIX process groups. The full 926-route script clean room regenerated 100
artifacts byte-identically, and the 237-test binding runtime/codegen matrix plus
the complete War Battles package check pass against the rebuilt extension.

and an optional C prefix. Local extension trees and Bob-resolved dependency

compiler methods, retain class-template specialization blockers, and prevent a
C++ free-function prefix from truncating method member names. The same tests
pin the established C numeric IDs and compile qualified C++ enum transport.

evidence; it does not claim that a method receiver or template specialization
has been materialized yet.
